## Game roster identifier issues

- Duplicate game ISO-2 `SD`: Republik sudan, Sudan selatan.
- Duplicate game ISO-2 `WS`: Samoa, Samoa amerika.
The output `countryId` is a composite of game ID, source ISO-2, and normalized game name so all 212 source records remain unique.

## Region geometry findings

The 150 region shapes are capture groups generated by dissolving Natural Earth Admin-1 features on the source region/region_cod attribute (or feature name when absent), then linking city markers. They are not inherently ADM1 units. Only unique exact name/code matches are linked; no centroid/overlap guess is treated as identity.

For unmapped region groups, centroid or largest-overlap allocation was not used to claim administrative identity: a dissolved capture shape may cover multiple ADM1 units. A centroid/overlap result would only be a spatial guess, not proof that the region is a province. The unmapped regions remain explicitly separate until an authoritative mapping exists.

## Mapping and source limitations

- CityCell assignment tries exact point-in-polygon, then nearest ADM1 boundary within 25 km (ties within 0.5 km are rejected), then unique normalized name.
- Region assignment is exact normalized name/code only. These region geometries are dissolved capture groups and are not assumed to be administrative provinces.
- ISO-only subdivision codes are reported, not counted as provinces, because the ISO code list does not identify their hierarchy and Natural Earth has no matching ADM1 feature.
- Natural Earth-only features are listed as candidates with internal IDs, are not represented as official ISO province codes, and cause a `conflict` status.
- `noSubdivision: true` means both input datasets contain no subdivision candidate; the single placeholder is not an actual province.
